### Identity (reviewed)

Rebecca Heineman preserves her disassembled and rewritten 1990 Apple IIgs Space Ace, replacing floppy-only ProDOS access with GS/OS hard-disk loading.
- https://github.com/olde-skuul/spaceaceiigs/blob/10ea4b36ea856be36f9f18314bf318cdedf2d6de/LICENSE.txt
- https://github.com/olde-skuul/spaceaceiigs/blob/10ea4b36ea856be36f9f18314bf318cdedf2d6de/source/spaceace.a65
- https://github.com/olde-skuul/spaceaceiigs/blob/10ea4b36ea856be36f9f18314bf318cdedf2d6de/source/custombuild.py
- https://github.com/olde-skuul/spaceaceiigs/blob/10ea4b36ea856be36f9f18314bf318cdedf2d6de/README.rst
- https://github.com/Olde-Skuul/spaceaceiigs/commits/10ea4b36ea856be36f9f18314bf318cdedf2d6de

### Classification (reviewed)

Disassembly-derived source restoration and GS/OS adaptation; source/spaceace.a65 contains the game flow, script loading, input and scoring.
- https://github.com/olde-skuul/spaceaceiigs/blob/10ea4b36ea856be36f9f18314bf318cdedf2d6de/LICENSE.txt

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The reconstructed original IIgs listing uses PHK/PLB, TSC/TDC, PEA and JSL: 65816.
- https://github.com/olde-skuul/spaceaceiigs/blob/10ea4b36ea856be36f9f18314bf318cdedf2d6de/LICENSE.txt

### Target CPU (reviewed)

The a65816 build emits native 65816 application/resource files, retaining the IIgs target.

### Build (reviewed)

source/custombuild.py invokes a65816 via the burger Python helpers. Upstream reports a Mac build and Sweet16/KEGS runs; these are author claims. Limitations: No candidate software was installed, assembled, compiled, executed, played or byte-compared. All four independent build flags remain null.

### Relationships (reviewed)

The author documents her own historical disassembly, not a mirror of another tracker project. ### Identity (reviewed)

Jawaid Bazyar’s pipeline rebuilds Ms. Pac-Man Z80 code, lifts it to C, then lowers gameplay to native 65816 with IIgs display/audio.

### Relationships (reviewed)

Derived from BleuLlama/GameDocs/Scott Lawrence’s disassembly, but adds a substantive Z80→C→65816 implementation. Original ROM/art rights remain reserved. Retained ROM bytes supply tables/data while native code implements gameplay; emulator harnesses are excluded from the promoted identity. Root, title and lineage were screened against the 1,876-project catalogue and 2,249 prior reviewed roots; no same implementation is already represented. The existing JOTD mspacman500 record is an Amiga implementation; this independently lowered 65816 source is a different implementation despite the same historical game and related arcade disassembly ancestry.

### Identity (reviewed)

Webifi’s IIgs Doom is a substantial native 65816 implementation of shareware episode-one gameplay with software rendering, sound, menus and saves.
- https://github.com/webifi/iigs-doom/blob/e645893f3d1ac6826e560e0ed3b28e0062c33995/LICENSE
- https://github.com/webifi/iigs-doom/blob/e645893f3d1ac6826e560e0ed3b28e0062c33995/BUILD.md

- https://github.com/Webifi/iigs-doom/commits/e645893f3d1ac6826e560e0ed3b28e0062c33995

### Classification (reviewed)

Source-derived native port. README credits Doom8088 inspiration and conversion from C; current Makefile explicitly builds all-65816 assembly without a C compiler/library.
- https://github.com/webifi/iigs-doom/blob/e645893f3d1ac6826e560e0ed3b28e0062c33995/LICENSE

The upstream Doom8088 README identifies DOS PC origins and 8088/286 targets; this is the source lineage, not the IIgs output architecture.

### Build (reviewed)

BUILD.md names Calypsi 5.18, make and Python 3.9+. It builds floppy/hard-disk images from included shareware episode-one data; other IWAD/PWAD support is explicitly absent. Claimed reproducible image matching was not checked. Limitations: No candidate software was installed, assembled, compiled, executed, played or byte-compared. All four independent build flags remain null.

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Upstream requires 4 MiB RAM (8 recommended) and AppleSqueezer GS or a 12 MHz ZipGS/TransWarp GS with at least 32 KiB cache. Its earlier 8 MiB/12 MHz emulator benchmark is not the minimum. Author reports real-hardware success after fixes, unverified here.

A native IIgs recreation of Avalon Hill’s Atari 8-bit Combat Chess, including ten captured boards, turn rules, local computer opponents and optional network play.

### Identity (reviewed)

The creator’s repository contains six native Apple II games, including original Arkabreakout/ChromaBreak and ports of POM1 games. Reviewed concrete Arkabreakout gameplay assembly, Snake C implementation, ChromaBreak bootstrap/build, and Chess documentation. This is one repository-level collection record, not six inflated entries.
- https://github.com/habib256/pom2games/blob/29909a924977e84636b4000d7341beafb6e3d288/README.md
- https://github.com/habib256/pom2games/blob/29909a924977e84636b4000d7341beafb6e3d288/arkabreakout/src/game.s

### Classification (reviewed)

Native Apple II source collection and ports. Arkabreakout assembles a standalone BRUN binary and DOS 3.3 disk; ChromaBreak builds a ProDOS system file. The bundled a2run/a2shot emulator code serves tests and is not embedded in the games. LOGO, demos and generic dev tooling are incidental to this game-scoped record.

The README and Snake header identify POM1 Apple-1/GEN2 versions as predecessors, and Chess documents reused POM1 rules code. Those predecessor files were not independently inspected, so no source CPU is inferred from the target assembly or Apple-1 label.

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Arkabreakout’s game.s explicitly specifies NMOS 6502 and contains native gameplay assembly. ChromaBreak’s Makefile passes --cpu 65c02; boot.s starts with a 6502 CPU probe and then selects .setcpu "65C02", rejecting an NMOS IIe. These establish separate 6502 and 65C02 outputs, not a single machine minimum.

The reviewed game Makefiles invoke ca65/ld65 or cl65 and construct bootable DOS 3.3/ProDOS disks. The README claims deterministic rebuilds and emulator-based tests, but those claims were not independently reproduced; byte_exact remains null. Limitations: Static primary-source review only. ### Relationships (reviewed)

The README and source headers explicitly derive the classic games from VERHILLE Arnaud’s POM1 work. Chess credits cc65-Chess for board/piece art and its Apple II port, but identifies its engine as POM1 rules code; this is not another copy of the already catalogued cc65-chess-stewbc-native. GPL-3.0 code, Skinner Microban levels, CC-BY-3.0 sprites and separate Apple ROM/DOS rights are disclosed. No blanket original-authorship or permissive-license claim is made. Normalized roots were screened programmatically against prior-roots.json before file review; current catalogue titles/subjects were checked for aliases. This is bounded lineage review, not proof of complete originality.

### Identity (reviewed)

Paul Wasson’s Where’s Askey? is the repository’s named 2021 contest entry, not generic text-game tooling. Its large tg.asm implements tile maps, interactions, stateful quests and the dog-rescue ending.

### Classification (reviewed)

Original native homebrew game source using Apple II text-mode tiles and page flipping. An Ultima-like description is genre inspiration; the inspected implementation does not claim recovered Ultima code.
- https://github.com/pmwasson/text-base-game/blob/c8c16fbe4b2cfaec68f61b4bc7ba3f5e2b09e4d8/README.md

### Source CPU (not-applicable)

The inspected author-owned native game source is newly authored homebrew, not an analysed predecessor executable. No original-binary CPU/platform is assigned from the target machine or genre inspiration; source fields remain empty.

### Target CPU (reviewed)

tg.asm contains native LDA/STA/JSR gameplay routines; tg.bat assembles/links it with ca65/cl65 -t apple2. This establishes the 6502 output family, without proving the minimum machine capable of all display features.
- https://github.com/pmwasson/text-base-game/blob/c8c16fbe4b2cfaec68f61b4bc7ba3f5e2b09e4d8/README.md

### Build (reviewed)

tg.bat builds the game with cc65 and AppleCommander and packages both ProDOS and DOS 3.3 images. Paths assume a Windows installation, and the disk images are supplied. No independent build or execution was performed. Limitations: Static primary-source review only. No candidate code, build, emulator, gameplay, hardware test or byte comparison was executed; all independent build flags remain null.

Pickman is Paul Wasson’s double-hires mining game. The inspected pickman.asm contains movement, digging, map generation and inventory logic; recent commits add shop items and dynamite/oil-barrel work.

Substantial original native homebrew game source, with a double-buffered DHGR engine and asset loader. It is not a generic graphics-tool record, even though DHGR Toolbox supplied the artwork.

### Identity (reviewed)

Octopede is the author’s Centipede-like Apple II game. The current README says single hi-res and identifies release 1.0 as the older low-res KansasFest 2019 version; the root description alone is stale.
- https://github.com/option8/octopede/blob/968f790c8a1e47d1e5af72cafe83eea291422add/README.md
- https://github.com/option8/octopede/blob/968f790c8a1e47d1e5af72cafe83eea291422add/OCTOPEDE.s
- https://github.com/option8/octopede/blob/968f790c8a1e47d1e5af72cafe83eea291422add/LOADER.s

### Classification (reviewed)

Native homebrew arcade implementation, with assembly for player/enemy movement, collision, scores and sprites. Its Centipede inspiration is not evidence of a disassembly or a copy of Atari game code.

The inspected author-owned native game source is newly authored homebrew, not an analysed predecessor executable. No original-binary CPU/platform is assigned from the target machine or genre inspiration; source fields remain empty.

### Target CPU (reviewed)

OCTOPEDE.s and LOADER.s use native 6502 instruction sequences and Apple II ROM/soft-switch addresses; the game explicitly includes a Datasoft RND 6502 routine. These establish 6502-family output, not a minimum Apple model.
- https://github.com/option8/octopede/blob/968f790c8a1e47d1e5af72cafe83eea291422add/README.md

The tree provides Merlin-style DSK/ORG/EQU assembly and an OCTOPEDE disk image, but no reviewed reproducible build script or assembler-version recipe. The last commits identify a version-2.0 hi-res update. Compilation and disk-image execution remain unknown. Limitations: Static primary-source review only. No candidate code, build, emulator, gameplay, hardware test or byte comparison was executed; all independent build flags remain null.

README explicitly requires a joystick. The current source is hi-res and the loader calls Apple disk/ROM routines. No precise Apple model, RAM, OS version or independently verified minimum was established. Limitations: Profiles preserve directly observed requirements/author claims only; no independent runtime hardware validation was performed.

### Identity (reviewed)

Justin Stahlman preserves his childhood rope-bridge action game under its original misspelling Archiologist. The README identifies the Indiana Jones scene as inspiration; ARCHIOLOGIST.BAS contains drawing, movement, enemies, rescue and end-song logic.
- https://github.com/stahlmandesign/archiologist-apple-ii/blob/5689f38b0b6034b3b4fd56fddd093968f986d805/README.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Original historical game-source preservation, not a reverse-engineered movie tie-in or emulator. The record covers the surviving listing and media, with no claim of recovered commercial source.
- https://github.com/stahlmandesign/archiologist-apple-ii/blob/5689f38b0b6034b3b4fd56fddd093968f986d805/README.md
- https://github.com/stahlmandesign/archiologist-apple-ii/blob/5689f38b0b6034b3b4fd56fddd093968f986d805/ARCHIOLOGIST.BAS

### Source CPU (no-evidence-found)

The surviving material is an Applesoft BASIC listing. It calls an external routine at address 770 but does not supply enough identified native bytes to establish that routine’s CPU. source_cpu remains empty rather than assigning the Apple II’s usual processor.
- https://github.com/stahlmandesign/archiologist-apple-ii/blob/5689f38b0b6034b3b4fd56fddd093968f986d805/README.md
- https://github.com/stahlmandesign/archiologist-apple-ii/blob/5689f38b0b6034b3b4fd56fddd093968f986d805/ARCHIOLOGIST.BAS

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Most gameplay is interpreted BASIC, but lines 1010–1095 explicitly POKE bytes 169 and 76 into $0300/$0302 and CALL 768, forming a 6502 LDA-immediate/JMP-to-$FCA8 delay stub. The 6502 target attribution is limited to that embedded native routine, not inferred from BASIC itself.
- https://github.com/esphynox/exit/blob/95218d61082ca7b483c715fe8b195eba3cf9c36a/README.md

- https://github.com/esphynox/exit/blob/95218d61082ca7b483c715fe8b195eba3cf9c36a/LICENSE

### Build (reviewed)

The shell script tokenizes BASIC and converts PNG graphics into a ProDOS 1.1.1 .po image with AppleCommander/tohgr. Static mismatches remain: it asks for EXIT.bas while the file is exit.bas, refers to .HGR after creating .hgr, and README says sh although the script uses arrays. No build was run to resolve these portability issues. Limitations: Static primary-source review only. No candidate code, build, emulator, gameplay, hardware test or byte comparison was executed; all independent build flags remain null.

Native BASIC homebrew adaptation of a named older game design. The source credits Shakya Development and Varona; no recovered original binary or emulator-wrapper execution is claimed.

The author’s original disassembly is retained in schram_v1; the canonical repository incorporates BradHawthorne’s contribution through merged PR #1, so that contributor fork is the same research lineage. Metadata reports a non-fork root and the author’s own provenance is stronger evidence than that flag alone.

- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-rescue-raiders-disasm/blob/422b7b515d04eab89f39ba585dc77f3405265e32/src/README.md

### Classification (reviewed)

The project reconstructs boot/loader code, selected runtime overlays and assets in maintained ca65 source. It is a native original-platform recovery, not a browser game built around a guest CPU interpreter.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-rescue-raiders-disasm/blob/422b7b515d04eab89f39ba585dc77f3405265e32/src/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-rescue-raiders-disasm/blob/422b7b515d04eab89f39ba585dc77f3405265e32/DISASSEMLY.md

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The recovery brief explicitly identifies Apple II 6502 code and the boot source uses .setcpu "6502" with native Disk II loading instructions.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-rescue-raiders-disasm/blob/422b7b515d04eab89f39ba585dc77f3405265e32/DISASSEMLY.md

The maintained boot source explicitly selects 6502 and the Makefile drives original Apple II disk reconstruction; Python is the host recovery tool, not the output CPU.

- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-rescue-raiders-disasm/blob/422b7b515d04eab89f39ba585dc77f3405265e32/Makefile

### Build (reviewed)

Upstream reports a verified byte-identical full disk rebuilt by reinserting 13 source-exact artifacts covering 41,216 stored-sector bytes into extracted sectors. The completion audit leaves selector-1 supporting loads and selector-3/4 loads unpromoted, and runtime checks approval-gated. Those are upstream reports only. All four build flags remain unknown because no independent build, byte comparison or runtime test was performed in this review. Limitations: Byte identity includes retained extracted sectors and does not mean the whole game has been converted to maintained source. No build or emulator was run in this review.

- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/assets/provenance.json

### Classification (reviewed)

Address-preserving ca65 game reconstruction plus lossless JSON assets and exact disk generation. The optional model viewer is an inspection aid; gameplay output remains the original Apple II program rather than a game-specific emulation wrapper.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/docs/status.md

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The recovered TITLE.2 decoder selects 6502 and contains native A/X/Y and zero-page operations. Other program/overlay files are reconstructed ca65 code; the launcher is Applesoft BASIC.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/src/title_2/title_2.s

### Target CPU (reviewed)

- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/Makefile
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/README.md

### Build (reviewed)

Upstream verification reports passing 17 tests, all 35 payload hashes, exact-image identity and a fresh second build. Apple2ts tests cover startup, menu, briefing, controls and a fixture-driven level transition; independent MAME reaches gameplay input. Those author-reported checks are preserved as evidence only. All four build flags remain unknown because this review did not independently build, compare or run the output. Limitations: Not rerun here. Full seven-system completion, save/high-score persistence, audio and joystick fidelity remain untested; Ctrl-R self-destruct is unresolved. Exact mode preserves original DOS/reserved/slack data, not reconstructed OS source.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/docs/verification.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/docs/status.md

### Runtime profiles (no-evidence-found)

Upstream tests an Apple II+ configuration in MAME and a separate Apple2ts scenario, but does not establish minimum CPU/RAM requirements. These tested configurations are documented without inferring a minimum profile.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/docs/verification.md

### AI (reviewed)

Commit 60dadd0 explicitly attributes the recovery to "single turn disassembly by astra"; status notes refer to installed Codex skills. AI usage is evidenced, but no interface label is inferred from the model-name attribution, so tools stays empty.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/blob/dcd1d8720697728cdefb14d6b6af0b355c775964/docs/status.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-stella7-disasm/commit/60dadd066a8526f2d28d63fdcdf925daf16591fa

### Relationships (reviewed)

Distinct from the already tracked Andy McFadden SourceGen project: that project covers the official/4am release with an early ROCK3 loader, whereas this supplied disk includes an added TITLE.2 and different payload layout. Inspected bootstrap.py/recover.py generate ca65 source directly from assets/raw binaries with their own control-flow renderer and no SourceGen import. The root is non-fork; no borrowed SourceGen source is credited. This supports an independent reconstruction, not a format-converted mirror.

README explicitly links Oric4ever/oric_sargon3. Its README and patcher.c confirm the Oric release patches the same Apple II binary and adds hardware adaptations, rather than reconstructing another independent code lineage. This pass counts the lineage once at this disassembly root.

### Identity (reviewed)

README, source index and final delivery receipt identify reconstruction of two supplied Apple II SunDog disks. There is no license grant in the pinned root tree. The exact 488-byte unidentified-data exception remains prominently disclosed.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/src/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/reference/final-build-validation.json

### Classification (reviewed)

This rebuilds original 6502 native routines and the historical SYSTEM.APPLE p-code runtime, with ca65 macros reproducing Pascal p-code and data. It does not introduce a modern embedded CPU emulator or translate the game to guessed high-level Pascal.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/src/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/runtime-architecture.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/HANDOFF.md

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The source index identifies native 6502 instructions and UCSD Pascal p-code. The 6502 is the physical processor; p-code is a virtual instruction set implemented by the historical runtime, not a second physical source CPU.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/src/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/runtime-architecture.md

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Source-built native bootstrap/runtime components are explicitly ca65 6502, with p-code reconstructed as byte-accurate instruction macros for that original runtime. Output is two native Apple II disks; no modern host CPU is inferred.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/src/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/runtime-architecture.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/reference/final-build-validation.json

### Build (reviewed)

Final receipt records two isolated strict builds without originals/prior outputs, byte-identical disk hashes, and 184,828 source-built, 101,404 identified-data and 488 user-assumed-data bytes. Handoff reports 180 passing tests. MAME and Apple2TS baseline checks cover play entry, disk swaps, utilities and save/fresh reload. These are upstream receipts, not independent verification by this review. All four build flags remain unknown because no candidate build, comparison or runtime test was performed here. Limitations: No candidate code was run here. The 488-byte classification is expressly an accepted assumption, not proved data; source-built totals include declarations. Combat/travel/audio/timing are not exhaustive, and final identical outputs reuse earlier behavioral baselines.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/reference/final-build-validation.json
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/HANDOFF.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/save-reload-validation.md

### Runtime profiles (no-evidence-found)

MAME evidence uses an unenhanced Apple IIe with Disk II and joystick; Apple2TS used another model. These are test configurations, not an established minimum CPU/RAM requirement, so no runtime minimum is inferred.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/save-reload-validation.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/docs/delivery-checklist.md

### AI (no-evidence-found)

The reviewed documentation, source-tree names and latest available commit messages contain no explicit development-AI attribution. Usage remains unknown; neither prose style nor lack of an instruction file establishes non-use.
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/README.md
- https://github.com/iolo/apple2-sundog-disasm/blob/41371a5142467977727c1f3ee380e4dd435a9bc0/HANDOFF.md

PRD credits the tracked laanwj/sundog Atari ST SDL work as a reference alongside Wizardry Apple Pascal research, and links the author’s other Apple II recoveries. The inspected native/p-code source index and Apple II disk provenance establish a separate reconstruction target and implementation, not a fork or platform wrapper of the ST interpreter.

### Source CPU (reviewed)

src/complete.s explicitly selects 6502 and defines Apple II keyboard, speaker, paddle and language-card switches around the recovered FS2 chunks.
- https://github.com/a2stuff/fs2/blob/447a620ea5cbe5f5f91e7e129b12c3d339653930/src/complete.s

### Target CPU (reviewed)

The native assembler source selects 6502 and Makefile passes --target apple2 to ca65. The optional binary target packages chunks with qkumba’s ProRWTS2; host assembler/compressor choice is not a modern output architecture.
- https://github.com/a2stuff/fs2/blob/447a620ea5cbe5f5f91e7e129b12c3d339653930/src/complete.s
- https://github.com/a2stuff/fs2/blob/447a620ea5cbe5f5f91e7e129b12c3d339653930/Makefile

### Build (reviewed)

README documents ca65/ld65 make, make validate comparisons of five chunks, and an optional ACME/apultra binary target. The resulting binary still requires an adjacent full 140-KB scenery disk file; README describes transferring it to the bundled ProDOS image. This inspection found no current test-result receipt, so compilable/run/play/byte-exact remain unknown rather than treating the comparison target as a verified pass. Limitations: The loading-panel chunk is copied raw; game/source-chunk buildability and full runnable scenery-backed output are distinct. No tools were installed or candidate code executed.
- https://github.com/a2stuff/fs2/blob/447a620ea5cbe5f5f91e7e129b12c3d339653930/README.md
- https://github.com/a2stuff/fs2/blob/447a620ea5cbe5f5f91e7e129b12c3d339653930/Makefile

### Runtime profiles (no-evidence-found)

README establishes a scenery-image dependency and the memory map uses language-card banking, but neither supplies an explicit verified minimum CPU/RAM profile. No minimum is inferred.
- https://github.com/a2stuff/fs2/blob/447a620ea5cbe5f5f91e7e129b12c3d339653930/README.md
- https://github.com/a2stuff/fs2/blob/447a620ea5cbe5f5f91e7e129b12c3d339653930/src/README.md

### AI (no-evidence-found)

The reviewed documentation, source-tree names and latest available commit messages contain no explicit development-AI attribution. Usage remains unknown; neither prose style nor lack of an instruction file establishes non-use.

### Relationships (reviewed)

README expressly credits qkumba’s ProDOS port and loader as the analyzed version; the September 19 commit records a corrected ProRWTS2 supplied by qkumba. This is a separately authored commented disassembly of original FS2 chunks, while the credited loader is retained as a dependency, not claimed as original research.

## Research event notes

Third Apple II/IIgs games pass, prepared against main 85209b21f550d6a3ac59033331a65374a70d8d25 (1,876 projects). Current catalogue, source/decision indexes and both previous Apple II reports supplied 2,249 normalized prior roots for screening before source review. Titles, aliases and code lineage were also checked; root collections count once and unchanged imports/platform patches do not add projects. Independent native implementations and independently reconstructed disassemblies remain distinct. Primary game, build, rights and bounded recent-commit evidence is recorded. Source and target CPUs are assessed independently; author-tested configurations are not silently converted to minima. No candidate software was installed, built, run, played or byte-compared; all independent build flags remain unknown. AI attribution follows explicit author statements or implementation coauthor evidence. Existing records, audit objects and activity are preserved.
